**Reading limits**

The original SPP field ledger calls the any-address route “unvalidated.” The later [configuration-refusal evidence](./gauge-configuration-refusals.md) establishes address-format validation and waiting for recognition; [Governance designer](../../governance/governance-designer.md#adding-a-body) owns that current bounded statement. The old phrase is retained only as historical evidence. Storybook examples and component defaults do not establish the rendered application behavior.

**Safe recognition heuristic**

Original home: [accounts/connecting-a-safe.md](../../application/connecting-a-safe.md). Original provenance: product-owner briefing on multisig-gated advancement into Token Voting (2026-07-28, see log.md) + app source verification (2026-08-05, app@122f1bd1; see log.md) + product-owner briefings (2026-09-11, see log.md) + Safe connection verification at app@adad67873c8f9dd75e3ed340b70df3e985ae3557 with locked Reown 1.8.23, wagmi connectors 8.1.0, Safe Apps SDK 9.1.0 and provider 0.18.6; official Safe guidance and public HTTP checks (2026-09-13, see log.md)

> **How the product recognizes a Safe**
>
> Recognizing an address as a Safe is a separate concern from connecting as one, and it fires elsewhere: in the Aragon team's [advanced-governance setup](../../governance/governance-designer.md#adding-a-body), through the add-body **any address** option, where any address can be registered as an external [body](../../governance/body.md). The product does not probe the contract's interface. It fetches the contract's name from a backend contract-metadata lookup, lowercases it, and tests whether it contains any of four fixed substrings — `Safe`, `GnosisSafe`, `Gnosis Safe`, `SafeProxy` (the comparison is case-insensitive on both sides). That is a naming heuristic standing in for a Safe-specific check, so it is fallible in both directions — an [honest-abstraction](../design/principles/honest-abstraction.md) gap between the product's "is this a Safe" model and onchain reality.
>
> A genuine Safe deployed under a contract name that doesn't contain any of those substrings goes unrecognized: the add-body field reports "Address type detected" instead of "Safe contract detected," the body gets none of the Safe branding described in [Safe as a body](../../governance/safe-as-a-body.md#how-the-app-presents-it), and — concretely — it drops out of the proposal-creation step's list of bodies eligible for create-proposal permission entirely, with no [`SafeOwnerCondition`](../../protocol-doc/helpers/condition-library/safe-owner-condition.md) offered for it; there is nothing to toggle even if that was meant to be the Safe's role. Conversely, a non-Safe contract whose name happens to contain one of those substrings (e.g. a vault contract named `SafeVault`) is treated as a Safe: it gets the Safe logo and branding, and becomes eligible for the `SafeOwnerCondition`-gated proposal-creation toggle. Whether that wiring can be published then turns on the contract rather than its name: the condition binds only to a target that answers `isOwner` ([the shape check, not an identity proof](../../protocol-doc/helpers/condition-library/safe-owner-condition.md)).

**SPP field mapping**

Original home: [governance/stage.md](../../governance/stage.md). Original provenance: verbal product-owner briefing on governance flows (2026-07-16, see log.md) + product-owner briefings (2026-07-28, see log.md) + product-owner principles review (2026-07-29, see log.md) + SPP and app source verification (2026-08-03, see log.md) + product-owner briefing on optimistic governance (2026-08-04, see log.md) + product-owner briefings (2026-09-11, see log.md) + classification verification at app@adad67873c8f9dd75e3ed340b70df3e985ae3557 (2026-09-13, see log.md)

> **SPP field disposition**
>
> The app's semantic layer does not mirror the lower-level structs one for one: some values are user-facing concepts, some are derived from those concepts, and some are fixed implementation choices. Against the pinned SPP parameter surface, every field is dispositioned as follows:
>
> | SPP field | Disposition in the app semantic layer |
> |---|---|
> | `Stage.bodies` | **Covered** — a stage contains zero or more bodies; zero bodies produces the timelock shape. |
> | `Stage.maxAdvance` | **Derived** — stage duration plus the optional expiration period; without an expiration the app uses a large default deadline. |
> | `Stage.minAdvance` | **Derived** — zero only when early advance is enabled for a nonempty approval-only stage; otherwise it equals the stage duration. |
> | `Stage.voteDuration` | **Covered** — stage duration. It is the automatic bodies' voting length and, when any body can veto, the protected veto window. |
> | `Stage.approvalThreshold` | **Covered** — the number of approving bodies required for the stage to pass. |
> | `Stage.vetoThreshold` | **Covered** — the number of vetoing bodies required to block the stage; a veto threshold, once met, overrides approvals. |
> | `Stage.cancelable` | **Fixed, not surfaced** — the app encodes `false`; app-created stages do not enable SPP proposal cancellation. |
> | `Stage.editable` | **Fixed, not surfaced** — the app encodes `false`; app-created stages do not enable SPP action editing. |
> | `Body.addr` | **Covered** — selecting or adding the body supplies its address, including the designer's unvalidated any-address route. |
> | `Body.isManual` | **Derived** — external-address bodies are manual; bodies installed by the flow are automatic. |
> | `Body.tryAdvance` | **Fixed, not surfaced** — the app encodes `true`; the report requests a same-transaction advance, which SPP still treats as best-effort and subject to state and permission checks. |
> | `Body.resultType` | **Covered** — each body's approval or veto role; both roles may appear in the same stage. |

**Unused status identifier**

Original home: [governance/proposal-status.md](../../governance/proposal-status.md). Original provenance: Universal Proposal Status flowchart + app repo proposalStatusUtils + product-owner briefings (2026-07-28, see log.md) + product-owner principles review (2026-07-29, see log.md) + app source verification (2026-08-05, app@122f1bd1; see log.md)

> The twelfth name is **FAILED**, and no derivation in the app produces it — not the shared tree, not any process type's inputs, not the stage-level tree — so no proposal and no stage ever reads FAILED. The vocabulary gives it a label and a color, so it is a status the app is equipped to display but can never arrive at, and one no user ever sees.

**Voting Terminal source assembly and reference stories**

Original home: [design/voting-terminal.md](../../governance/proposal.md#voting). Original provenance: product-owner briefings (2026-07-28, see log.md) + product-owner release-notes briefing (2026-08-03, see log.md) + gov-ui-kit@9c106d8 and app@122f1bd1 source verification (2026-08-04, see log.md) + classification verification at app@adad67873c8f9dd75e3ed340b70df3e985ae3557 (2026-09-13, see log.md)

> **Hierarchy**
>
> The pattern has a stable order: **process status first**, **body selection second**, **decision detail third**. `ProposalVoting` supplies the tab shell for **Breakdown**, **Votes**, and **Settings**; the app assembly supplies each panel's threshold explanation, participation list, settings, and any action control, and may omit a tab explicitly. `ProposalVoting` renders app-provided status inputs and does not calculate a combined stage result or advance a stage; the staged assembly re-evaluates time-dependent stage status and timing before supplying them, and the summary text, body requirements, statuses, vote lists, settings, and action controls all come from the assembly.
>
> The four reference stories exercise the product shapes as follows:
>
> | Story | Shape and state |
> | --- | --- |
> | `SimpleGovernance` | One active proposal card with a single Token Voting body, live status, token breakdown, vote action, votes, and settings. |
> | `SingleStage` | One expired accordion stage with a single Multisig body; the completed decision remains available for inspection. |
> | `MultiStage` | Three sequential stages in one controlled accordion: active Token Holders, pending Founders, then a pending branded Safe body. |
> | `MultiBody` | One active stage opens on a two-row Token/Safe summary and an approval requirement; selecting a row drills into that body's own terminal, and the Safe body omits Votes. |
>
> **Implementation scope**
>
> These assembly identifiers ground the pattern in current source; they are not part of its public design-system API.
>
> | Assembly | Place in the pattern |
> | --- | --- |
> | `sppVotingTerminal` | The staged assembly. It replaces the shared terminal and composes the accordion, stage statuses, body summaries, mixed approval/veto bodies, timelocks, and advance action. |
> | `proposalVotingTerminal` | The shared non-staged, single-body fallback used by Token Voting, Multisig, and Lock to Vote. It composes one `ProposalVoting` card with the plugin's breakdown, vote action, vote list, and settings; unlike Storybook's `SingleStage` example, it does not render a `Stage` wrapper. |
> | `adminVotingTerminal` | An intentional empty-state stub carrying the automatic-execution notice ([the proposal details page](../../governance/proposal.md#proposal-details-page)). This agrees with the Admin exception that always produces `EXECUTED`; it is not another voting state. |
> | `gaugeVoterVotingTerminal` | Excluded. Despite the similar code name, it is the sticky epoch-allocation control on the dedicated [Gauge voting](../../governance/gauge-voting.md) page. Gauge Voter has no proposal lifecycle for this pattern to render. |

**Voting Terminal assembly details**

Original home: [design/voting-terminal.md](../../governance/proposal.md#voting). Original provenance: product-owner briefings (2026-07-28, see log.md) + product-owner release-notes briefing (2026-08-03, see log.md) + gov-ui-kit@9c106d8 and app@122f1bd1 source verification (2026-08-04, see log.md) + classification verification at app@adad67873c8f9dd75e3ed340b70df3e985ae3557 (2026-09-13, see log.md)

> **Assembly rules**
>
> - **Approval and veto are body roles.** Render each body's role and result treatment independently, show both thresholds in a mixed stage, and keep a veto body actionable until its window closes even after approval lands. Treating a stage as globally "approval" or globally "veto" is incorrect. The acceptance case is one Token Holder approval body and one Security Council veto body in the same stage.
> - **A manual body's role is configuration.** The role rendered for an external body is the role the app is configured to render; the protocol's manual report stays the authoritative result, and the terminal does not turn its label into an additional contract constraint.
> - **The timelock card belongs to the assembly.** The dedicated card, illustration, and named timelock states are the staged app assembly's; gov-ui-kit contributes the ordinary stage and advance-window status treatment.
> - **Advance display reads both bounds.** The design-system treatment takes the minimum and maximum advance times and derives the countdown, the advanceable copy within a 90-day window, and the absence of status after the maximum; the app keeps the advance action disabled until the minimum is reached. The display branch does not change the product meaning that the timing floor is part of becoming actionable.
> - **Hide Votes for external bodies deliberately.** The assembly may hide the Votes tab explicitly: the `MultiBody` reference does so, while `MultiStage` leaves the tab available; the staged app hides it for external bodies.

**Wizard container and footer ownership**

Original home: [design/wizard.md](../../application/wizard.md). Original provenance: product-owner briefing, 2026-07-20 (wizard system) + product-owner release-notes briefing (2026-08-03, see log.md) + gov-ui-kit and app source verification (2026-08-04, see log.md) + product-owner briefings (2026-08-04, see log.md) + interrupted-flows pass at app@122f1bd1 (2026-08-05, see log.md) + product-owner ruling (2026-08-07, see log.md) + product-owner briefings (2026-09-11, see log.md)

> A dialog wizard uses the general `Dialog` container; its wizard-control row is `Dialog.Footer variant="wizard"`, which places Back or Close on the left and the primary Next or Submit action on the right. This is a container and footer treatment, not a third dialog component. The consuming app owns the step graph, form state and validation, navigation, exit behavior, analytics, and transitions to other dialogs. The footer variant can also serve another bounded procedural dialog, so the variant alone does not make a flow a wizard; see the complete [dialog taxonomy](../design/dialog-taxonomy.md).

**Wizard exit implementation choices**

Original home: [design/wizard.md](../../application/wizard.md). Original provenance: product-owner briefing, 2026-07-20 (wizard system) + product-owner release-notes briefing (2026-08-03, see log.md) + gov-ui-kit and app source verification (2026-08-04, see log.md) + product-owner briefings (2026-08-04, see log.md) + interrupted-flows pass at app@122f1bd1 (2026-08-05, see log.md) + product-owner ruling (2026-08-07, see log.md) + product-owner briefings (2026-09-11, see log.md)

> **Leaving an unfinished wizard**
>
> Every wizard inherits one exit guard from the shared wizard root, so the behavior is the same in a full-screen wizard and in a dialog wizard; what it challenges and when it releases is documented on [Submitting a transaction](../../application/wizard.md#leaving-an-unfinished-wizard). Three design choices sit behind it. The challenge is the browser's own prompt rather than a product dialog, accepted as a low-cost implementation choice and not a taxonomy statement: the guard sits outside the [dialog taxonomy](../design/dialog-taxonomy.md), with no alert severity and no named forward action, and the product attaches no design intent to the prompt's presentation. The guard is the app's rule and the app's mechanism; gov-ui-kit supplies no exit-guard machinery. Dismissing a dialog wizard through its own close control is deliberately outside the guard, because a dialog wizard holds a bounded sub-flow with little content; those dialogs instead refuse outside-click dismissal, the app's own choice on top of a component-library default that would dismiss on an outside click.

**Transaction lifecycle ownership**

Original home: [design/transaction-submission.md](../../application/submitting-a-transaction.md). Original provenance: product-owner release-notes briefing (2026-08-03, see log.md) + app transaction-dialog verification (2026-08-04, see log.md) + interrupted-flows pass at app@122f1bd1 (2026-08-05, see log.md) + @aragon/app@1.36.0 retry-safety verification (2026-09-08, see log.md)

> **Ownership**
>
> The chain rule, the attempt memory, and the retry guard are the app's own logic; gov-ui-kit supplies the dialog container and no transaction lifecycle.

**Datalist library and application responsibilities**

Original home: [design/datalist-page.md](../../application/collection-pages.md). Original provenance: product-owner briefing, 2026-07-28 (transactions and datalist pages) + codebase verification at app@122f1bd1 (2026-08-06, see log.md) + product-owner ruling (2026-08-07, see log.md)

> **Design rules**
>
> - **Tabs are page-defined.** Each page declares the distinctions that matter to its collection; there is no universal partition copied across every datalist.
> - **Keep the three loading states apart, and let loading-more win.** First load, refreshing, and loading more say different things; a list fetching more is never presented as loading from scratch.
> - **Empty is derived, not declared.** The component library derives emptiness from the settled state plus the item count. Its separate filtered-to-nothing state, with its own copy and illustration, is opinionated toward search-style filtering, which no datalist page offers, so it stays deliberately unconnected; tab and chip narrowing shows the collection's ordinary empty copy.
> - **Scope a failure to what failed.** A page failure or a required-data failure replaces the page; a valid empty result replaces only the list region. A failed list gets the library's action slots left empty. The app switches the data layer's automatic retries off product-wide (three retries is that layer's own default), so a transient failure surfaces on the first attempt; in-place retry belongs to [transaction submission](../../application/submitting-a-transaction.md).
> - **Decline sort and search on these pages.** The component library ships both; no product surface renders the sort control, and the four collection pages render no search input. The asset picker's search and the [explore page](../../application/explore-page.md)'s search are not datalist pages.
> - **Load more, never paginate.** Appending the next page is the only pagination the library offers; each page's batch size doubles as its placeholder-row count.
> - **Collapse chrome that offers no choice.** The library collapses a tab bar that would hold exactly one tab; the app removes a panel whose tab set is empty, promotes a lone tab's label to the card title, and applies its own thresholds to the switcher and chip controls that carry a datalist page's tabs.
> - **Persist a selection in the URL, not in history.** The persistence, the clearing on leaving, and the fallback are the app's; the library's controls have no notion of the URL.

  | --- | --- | --- |

The current [adapter package](https://registry.npmjs.org/@reown/appkit-adapter-wagmi/-/appkit-adapter-wagmi-1.8.23.tgz), [AppKit package](https://registry.npmjs.org/@reown/appkit/-/appkit-1.8.23.tgz), [controllers package](https://registry.npmjs.org/@reown/appkit-controllers/-/appkit-controllers-1.8.23.tgz), [wagmi connectors](https://registry.npmjs.org/@wagmi/connectors/-/connectors-8.1.0.tgz), [Safe provider](https://registry.npmjs.org/@safe-global/safe-apps-provider/-/safe-apps-provider-0.18.6.tgz), and [Safe SDK](https://registry.npmjs.org/@safe-global/safe-apps-sdk/-/safe-apps-sdk-9.1.0.tgz) preserve the inspected implementations. Locked UI-kit versions are `2.9.0`/`2.11.4`; the UI kit does not supply this connector mechanism and was not re-audited.

  In app `apps/app/src/modules/application/constants/wagmi.ts`, the Wagmi adapter uses its default connector setup; `components/providers/providers.tsx` initializes AppKit before descendants render. In the adapter package, `dist/esm/src/utils/helpers.js:getSafeConnector` adds wagmi's `safe()` through `dist/esm/src/client.js:addThirdPartyConnectors`. Controllers' `CoreHelperUtil.isSafeApp` requires an iframe whose immediate `location.ancestorOrigins[0]` hostname is `app.safe.global`; absent browser information returns false. AppKit's `appkit-base-client.js:initialize` → `syncExistingConnection` → `syncNamespaceConnection` selects that connector; adapter `syncConnection` connects it without the modal. Wagmi `src/safe.ts:getProvider` obtains `sdk.safe.getInfo()` and creates `SafeAppProvider`. Provider `dist/provider.js` returns `safe.safeAddress` for `eth_accounts` and forwards `eth_sendTransaction` to `sdk.txs.send`; SDK `dist/esm/communication/index.js` uses `window.parent.postMessage` and message events. The compared versions retain this mechanism; the connector's optional-import handling differs without changing the transport. App `src/assets/locales/en.json:helpTextSafe` explicitly offers custom Safe App or WalletConnect QR, and `sppVotingTerminalBodyVoteDefault.tsx` checks the connected address against the external body. The independent WalletConnect branch starts at adapter `connectWalletConnect` and `setUniversalProvider`; shared app metadata is in `src/shared/constants/walletConnectDefinitions.ts`. Linked-account pairing and session requirements remain for `verify-direct-and-linked-account-execution`, whose source pointers now consume these findings.

  Checked official Safe [custom-app instructions](https://help.safe.global/articles/1436723729-add-a-custom-safe-app), [iframe/SDK integration guidance](https://help.safe.global/articles/6872363437-145503-how-to-create-a-safe-app-with-safe-apps-sdk-and-list-it), and [WalletConnect pairing instructions](https://help.safe.global/articles/6643739210-how-to-connect-a-safe-to-a-dapp-using-walletconnect) on 2026-09-13. Public HTTP requests to [Aragon](https://app.aragon.org) and its [manifest](https://app.aragon.org/manifest.json) returned 200; the manifest supplies Aragon's name/icon and `Access-Control-Allow-Origin: *`. Both responses include an enforcing CSP allowing `https://app.safe.global` as a frame ancestor, alongside `X-Frame-Options: DENY`; [CSP's frame-ancestor rule takes precedence](https://w3c.github.io/webappsec-csp/#frame-ancestors-and-frame-options) in conforming browsers. Source `proxyUtils.ts` agrees on the allowed Safe host; the deployed manifest's CORS header is an HTTP observation, not a claim that this source snapshot supplies every deployment header. These checks do not attest the deployed application revision, browser compatibility, a successful wallet session, or an executed transaction. No browser, signature, transaction, or contract tests were performed. The docs retain WalletConnect as the alternative when automatic embedded connection is unavailable; no browser support matrix or product defect was established.

Added bounded [recovery conditions](../../governance/process-removal.md#removing-the-last-recognized-process) and linked them from the ROOT self-call explanation in [OSx authorization paths](../../access-control/osx-authorization-paths.md#root-administers-the-dao-permission-table). Distinguished no recognized process, no unrestricted Execute, and no independently usable repair authority. Directly usable ROOT can restore Execute; independently usable upgrade authority can permit replacement code that repairs it. Account-self-held ROOT or upgrade authority cannot start that recovery after Execute is lost. The factory's default self-held permissions provide no independent rescuer.

- **2026-09-10** — Applied the owner's permanent prohibition on self-referential editorial prose in product documentation and requirement that unfinished documentation work live in `tasks/`. [WORKFLOW.md](../../WORKFLOW.md#product-content-and-documentation-operations) now owns the content boundary and destinations for provenance, history, review state, questions, verification, and source requests; [voice.md](../../voice.md) enforces it with `V-R018`, calibration examples, and a preflight for every added or changed paragraph. AGENTS.md routes all prose edits through those policies, and the canonical wiki-cli and release-reconciliation skills and ledger reference now apply them. Corrected the queued structural audits so purpose inventories do not become editorial page openings and owner questions stay in tasks. Converted the two existing review-only board rows into review-semantic-anchors and review-remaining-drafts without changing their cohorts, order, or prerequisites. The 19 existing page questions remain intact as an explicitly transitional inventory owned by the cleanup task; no new page-question copy is permitted. **Board delta:** two existing review rows converted to tasks — review-semantic-anchors (`ready`, next actor `owner`) and review-remaining-drafts (`blocked`, next actor `none`); clean-product-prose-and-route-open-questions narrowed to the remaining prose cleanup and question migration (`ready`, next actor `agent`); no new owner ask, gap, draft, opportunity, or page question.

The existing page-purpose and section-fit audits explicitly exclude sentence-level cleanup and retain their structural scope and prerequisites. No product prose, question, review status, or operating convention was changed in this commissioning pass. **Board delta:** task added — clean-product-prose-and-route-open-questions (`ready`, next actor `agent`, first in Ready to run); no owner ask, gap, draft, opportunity, or page question added or retired.

Corrected a contradictory sentence in the release-processing skill to keep release-note framing out of canonical articles, consistent with the owner's rule. **Board delta:** none.

- **2026-09-09** — Expanded the existing [APP-1152 bug](https://linear.app/aragon/issue/APP-1152/fix-missing-metadata-warnings-and-duplicate-proposal-titles) to cover both missing metadata warnings on staged-proposal cards in the process tab and All proposals, and the empty-title fallback repeating the proposal identifier already displayed on the card. Preserved the ticket's existing screenshot and example link. Retired show-metadata-warnings-on-every-proposal-list and its board row at the owner's request; Linear retains the bug scope, and current-product documentation remains unchanged pending implementation. **Board delta:** one opportunity retired after ticketing (16 → 15); no documentation task, draft, question, or knowledge gap changed.

- **2026-09-09** — Applied the owner's answers to [Aragon Notifications](../../governance/aragon-notifications.md) and [Cross-chain execution](../../governance/cross-chain-execution.md). Notifications is maintained in `app-backend` and sends exactly three alerts: proposal created, proposal ending in 24 hours, and proposal executed. The reminder timing is fixed across processes and subscriptions; any per-subscription customization waits for actual customer demand, with no opportunity commissioned. Each person may subscribe to 50 accounts. Stored data is limited to the Telegram user ID, subscribed accounts, and each subscription's mute state; subscriptions persist until removed, and unsubscribing from the last account removes the person's stored record. The four page questions are answered and deleted, and the dashboard, proposal page, and repository directory now reflect the service directly without the stale source-flag qualification.

  Cross-chain deployments are bespoke to each account, including controllers, executors, adapters, and routes. The owner ruled a shared deployment-address/build catalogue and deployment-specific monitoring or Aragon escalation guidance outside this page's scope; both questions and the recovery-support discussion are removed. Canonical prose now describes the supported composition and reading experience. - **2026-08-06** — Closed out document-page-and-list-state: one Opus evidence pass over `app@122f1bd1` (pivotal library behaviors verified against the installed gov-ui-kit 2.9.0 rather than the lagging 2.8.1 local checkout), two parallel writers, and one adversarial Opus review whose findings were applied, run in an isolated worktree and hand-merged onto the day's board reshape and the three concurrently completing passes. The review's two blockers guarded the same line: the filtered-to-nothing collapse must read as a non-decision, never a product choice. **Two of the task's own claims did not survive the code.** List filter state is not unpersisted — chip, tab, and switcher selections are written to the URL, restored on reload or from a shared link, and used deliberately as a cross-page hand-off (the dashboard's view-all links carry the selected body into the collection pages); what makes selection volatile is that the app deletes the parameter on leaving the page and writes it without history entries. And "never an in-place retry" holds only for read surfaces — transaction flows retry in six-plus places, so the page scopes the rule to reads and hands retry to [transaction submission](../../application/submitting-a-transaction.md).

  [Datalist page](../../application/collection-pages.md) now states the list-state vocabulary: three loading states kept apart (first load, refresh with rows on screen, loading more taking precedence); emptiness derived from state plus row count rather than a state of its own; and the filtered-to-nothing collapse stated as fact, with the component library's distinct filtered state and presentation named as built and unconnected — a non-decision, with the intent question flagged for the owner on the board rather than answered on the page. Failure gets the page-level not-found-versus-generic split (a failed list draws no distinction at all), the scope rule that a page failure replaces the page while a valid empty result replaces only the list region, and leave-or-support recovery with the app switching the query layer's three automatic retries off product-wide. Search and sort are written as the deliberate non-decision the task wanted intact — sort declined absolutely, search declined on the four collection pages with the asset-selection picker and the [explore page](../../application/explore-page.md) named as out-of-pattern surfaces, and the members order left with [token panel](../../governance/token-panel.md). Pagination is load-more as the library's only offer, with app-chosen batch sizes of 10/18/20/20; the library's exactly-one tab-bar collapse is attributed as library behavior beside the app's own rules — the zero-option guard, a lone tab's label promoted into the card title, and the two-or-more thresholds on the toggle switchers and chip rows that actually carry a datalist page's tabs.

  [Alert severity](../design/alert-severity.md) answers both of its original open questions in its body: advisory cards and inline advisories carry the library's four visual variants under the product's two-severity rule (the same toolkit-options-do-not-extend-the-rule move the dialog section already made); field errors are typed to warning and critical and hardcoded critical by the shared form layer; the app's banner strip carries no severity choice; the product has no toast mechanism — not a declined offer, since the library ships none either, and the only auto-dismissing notices belong to the third-party wallet SDK inside its own modal — and failure-versus-emptiness is answered as scope. The task's criterion that the page end with no `## Open questions` section predates the chain-mismatch question the interrupted-flows pass added; that question is not answered by this pass and stays open, so the section remains with exactly it. New draft [Dashboard](../../accounts/dashboard.md) (accounts, capability) states the fixed Proposals/Members/Assets order, the shared recognized-plugin gate on the first two with Assets ungated, the always-present details aside as the page's authoritative-record drill-down, the onboarding state that replaces header and main column (owned by [admin flow](../../governance/admin-flow.md)), the account-keyed header slot (mechanism with [DAO slots](../design/dao-slots.md)), and the three/six/three previews routing into the datalist pages; it is linked from the accounts map. [Every element makes a claim](../design/principles/every-element-makes-a-claim.md) gains a one-sentence empty-versus-failed pointer.

  The pre-reshape board's expectation that this row rewrites [abstract, then offer a drill-down](../design/principles/honest-abstraction.md#abstract-then-offer-a-drill-down) did not hold: the task file scoped that page as unblocked only, and its authoritative-fact definition would not admit the dashboard's preview-to-collection links as an instance — the dashboard page instead names its details aside as the pattern's real instance on that surface. Recorded without filing, three findings: the optimistic filter-chip policy (chips show while their availability probe is pending and stay on probe failure, disappearing only on a confirmed zero); an offline-paused read presenting as settled rather than loading; and the asset-selection picker's exact-match search (a term matches only the full token name or symbol, filtering runs client-side over already-loaded pages, and load-more is suppressed while a term is active) — the treasury pass retired its task before this one could route the fact there, and its pages do not cover that picker's search semantics, so it stays here for the owner to route. **Board delta:** task retired — document-page-and-list-state (row removed, file deleted); owner ask added — confirm the filtered-to-nothing collapse is intended (input row, last by unblock value); draft added — Dashboard (accounts), inventory 74 → 75; page questions resolved — two on alert severity, platform aggregate 20 across 17 pages; no knowledge gap or opportunity touched.

- **2026-08-05** — Ran document-governance-derivation-and-availability at `app@122f1bd1`: six parallel writers, one adversarial verification pass over the combined diff, one fix iteration, then a rebase-time reconciliation with the same-day owner-review passes — the advanced-availability correction this task originally carried had been retired from it by the owner's self-serve ruling, and nothing from it is re-stated. **The one remaining correction landed first.** `governance/staged-proposals.md` now distinguishes the advance flag the app configures at install time (the best-effort attempt, set for every body) from the one report transaction it constructs itself — a manual, non-plugin body's report, which never requests same-transaction advancement — so after such a report the stage waits for an explicit advance. `governance/proposal-status.md` names **FAILED** as the twelfth vocabulary member: defined with a label and color in gov-ui-kit 2.9.0 as installed, produced by no derivation in the app (shared tree, per-plugin wrappers, and stage-level tree all verified; only test mocks reference it) — a status the app is equipped to display but can never arrive at, a latent defect no user sees. `governance/proposal-creation.md` states the designer's defaults: eligibility preselected on Members with every listed body toggled on (plugin bodies and Safes; a manual body that is not a Safe never appears in the step), yielding a rule condition that ORs one membership condition per listed body on a staged process; on a basic process, a multisig installs restricted to its listed members, and Token Voting / Lock to Vote install a one-whole-token proposer minimum (held vs locked respectively). `governance/process.md` gains "Which processes a user sees": the visible set is derived — interpretable process plugins only, the App CMS per-account hide list, a feature-flag gate on linked-account processes — then deliberately ordered, primary account first, then Token Voting → Multisig → Lock to Vote → the rest, with ties keeping listing order. Cross-action coherence went to `governance/action.md`, not the pre-empt-protocol-rejected-configuration opportunity: that family is documented-invalid field values, while this is order-dependent batch coherence with no invalid value — the page now states that no validation layer checks a proposal's actions against each other, with the multisig membership/threshold case in both directions (a conflicting batch is composable and reverts whole at execution; a batch adding members cannot express the higher threshold they would make valid) and opt-in, non-blocking action simulation as the only whole-batch surface. Corrected in passing: `app-cms.md`'s further-feeds enumeration said two files where the code ships three — the third is the per-plugin-type address whitelist the governance designer's body-type picker reads. **Board delta:** task retired — document-governance-derivation-and-availability (row removed, file deleted); no owner ask, gap, opportunity, draft, or page question added or removed.

- **2026-08-05** — Closed out document-recognition-and-handoff-limits at `app@122f1bd1`, run as four parallel writer passes (two Sonnet for the deterministic lookups, two Opus for the judgement-heavy items) with orchestrator-level QA and one adversarial Opus review of the combined diff. The four honest-abstraction limits now sit on their pages, each verified first-hand against source rather than carried over from the audit — which mattered, because the audit's raw matrix had been cleared and two of its framings did not survive re-derivation (below). The adversarial review mattered too: it cut a dark-launched surface from the inventory (the basic flow's existing-conditions notice is behind a flag with no production entry), corrected the worked instance's account scope (four registrations fill that slot, not one), and pulled the Safe false-positive consequence back to the app layer, where it belongs — onchain, the condition itself refuses a target that cannot answer `isOwner`.

  **The four limits.** [Plugin](../../governance/plugin.md) states that a known plugin's integration depth varies and the floor is a single stand-alone page: Gauge and Capital Distributor register only the plugin-page slot, so no participation in proposals, no settings surface, no presence in the governance designer — named on the page as what the partially-supported delivery posture looks like in the interface. [Connecting a Safe](../../application/connecting-a-safe.md) states the recognition rule: the backend contract-metadata name, lower-cased, tested against four fixed substrings, at exactly one call site (the designer's add-body any-address field) — fallible in both directions, with the concrete consequence of each miss (an unrecognized genuine Safe silently drops out of the proposal-creation eligibility list entirely; a name-matched non-Safe gets Safe branding and the `SafeOwnerCondition` toggle). [DAO slots](../design/dao-slots.md) carries the worked instance its second use lacked: Cryptex's allocation-file generator, a DAO-slot control injected immediately above the Capital Distributor campaign-creation form's upload field that produces the very file the next field asks for. [Reach out when the abstraction cannot stay honest](../../application/getting-help.md#when-the-app-needs-the-aragon-team) carries the entry-point inventory: exactly three surfaces render the handoff — the designer's team-built advanced-governance panel, the dashboard onboarding card during the admin flow, and the explore getting-started card — all sending to the same external assistance form.

  **Two audit framings corrected by re-derivation.** The per-account second tier is not "branding plus embedded controls": only Cryptex ships both; katana (twice) and a Sepolia demo account ship controls with no branding. And the embedded control's production reachability holds for a non-obvious reason — the control lives in the action layer and surfaces through the generic permission-driven action composer; its only tie to the dark-launched capital-flow module is a bare slot-id enum with no runtime gate, so the flag that keeps that module out of production does not touch it, and the module's open owner question stays untouched and unnamed.

  **The inventory's sharper counter-finding.** The app also names limits where it renders no route out: an advanced process shows its composition read-only with no edit control and no contact entry point, and the partially-supported client call to action has no in-app entry point at all — the affected governance-type option appears disabled with a **By request** tag, without the remedy [control availability](../design/control-availability.md) says a disabled control owes. Ordinary support (the footer Support link, the error-state Report issue action) is stated on the page as a different kind; the in-app support widget stays out entirely, being an open owner question.

  **Corrected in passing.** [Governance designer](../../governance/governance-designer.md) claimed Safe recognition "checks via the Safe API"; no such call exists anywhere in the app, and the sentence now links the heuristic on Connecting a Safe. [DAO slots](../design/dao-slots.md)' former open question is answered from source and folded in: slot content is imported and hand-registered in the app's source, so a bespoke slot is Aragon-team work shipped with a release, with no configuration path — and the page's definition now admits that a slot can hold a resolver function as well as a component, which the Cryptex instance itself uses. The advanced-access gate discrepancy the handoff sweep independently re-found was settled the same day by the owner's availability answers (below), so nothing was re-filed; the inventory's advanced bullet and the reach-out framing follow that ruling — the boundary is a configuration the flow cannot represent faithfully, not advanced access itself.

  **Board delta:** task retired — document-recognition-and-handoff-limits (row removed, file deleted); page question answered and removed — DAO slots (how slot content ships), platform aggregate now 21 across 17 pages; no owner ask added or resolved (the capital-flow and support-widget rulings stay with their existing input row); no gap opened, closed, or narrowed; no draft added or removed.
- **2026-08-05** — Closed out document-treasury-composition-and-history at `app@122f1bd1`, run as four parallel verification passes (two Sonnet, two Opus) followed by an Opus writing pass and an adversarial Opus review. **The assets withdrawal claim is corrected, not cleared — the audit's counter-finding was right.** [Assets](../../treasury/assets.md) claimed a withdrawal is "a selection against real holdings, not an address paste"; the code offers both — the selector's default list is the account's indexed holdings with balances, and a permanently available **Add address** route accepts any address that resolves as an ERC-20, never checked against holdings and entering at a balance of zero. The amount is not capped by the balance, deliberately: the treasury transfer form switches off the max rule the same input enforces for token locking and wrapping. The page now states the default-not-boundary rule, with the balance-error claim scoped to the transfer path because lock and wrap do surface one.

  **The audit's truncation claim inverted under verification.** The truncating coercion it pointed at (`bigIntUtils`) is real but unreachable from the amount path; the amount is parsed by viem's `parseUnits`, which **rounds half-up** on the last retained digit and writes the parsed value back into the field, silently. [Create transaction](../../treasury/create-transaction.md) states the rounding rule with a worked example and names it a departure from both [refusal](../design/invariant-validation.md) and [meaning-preserving normalization](../design/input-normalization.md), whose table counts amount precision among what a conversion may change. That divergence is filed as the candidate [preserve or reject amounts the token cannot express](../product-opportunities/preserve-or-reject-over-precise-amounts.md). One adjacent finding is recorded here without filing: the same field accepts scientific notation such as `1e-7`, which the unit parser rejects on an unguarded render path, reaching an app-level error boundary rather than a field message — established by source reading only, not reproduced in a browser, so a reproduction should precede any ticket.

  **Tabs are conditional, and sharper than the audit claimed.** The tab row on [Transactions](../../treasury/transactions.md) renders only when at least two of executions, deposits, and withdrawals have history — zero or two-plus tabs, never one, with **All transactions** riding along rather than counting, and a failed probe counting as history; test-covered. The page states the rule and reconciles it with [datalist page](../../application/collection-pages.md)'s page-defined framing: the set is the page's model, its appearance follows the account's history. **Decode degradation is immediate on mismatch, bounded on polling.** Opening an execution polls the backend decode status every two seconds up to ten responses; a decoded/raw count mismatch degrades every row to raw calldata at once — including cleanly decoded ones — with the action count staying accurate; the bound is the dialog's own and is not generalized to proposal surfaces. The adversarial review mattered: it found the degradation is not silent (each degraded row carries the action viewer's "Action had issues with decoding" warning when expanded, so the page now scopes silence to the polling bound alone), that window expiry is not final (a stale reopen refetches and can pick up a completed decode), and that a claimed review step does not exist in the one-step flow — all three corrected before landing. **Board delta:** task retired — document-treasury-composition-and-history (row removed, file deleted); opportunity added — preserve-or-reject-over-precise-amounts (`candidate`, treasury); no owner ask, page question, gap, or draft added or removed.

- **2026-08-05** — Completed document-token-holder-prompts-and-reward-routes at `app@122f1bd1`, closing both ends of the token holder's economic loop — run as three parallel writers, one adversarial reviewer, and, after the task's mid-pass refinement raised the bar, a combined dialog test in the app repo. **The simultaneity case is settled by evidence rather than code reading:** a real two-watcher render under one real dialog provider (six assertions, five identical runs; all probe files removed afterwards) confirms that when the delegation and lock-or-wrap prompts are eligible together, exactly one shows — dialogs replace rather than stack, the survivor is decided by mount order and query timing rather than any rule, a replacement can remove a dialog the holder is already reading, and the lost prompt does not return until a fresh deliberate connection. The test also narrowed the reachability precondition (the exit-queue case collides only when the holder also retains a positive underlying balance) and showed the shared profile-pause flag releases both watchers in the same commit, making simultaneity more likely. Per the task's routing rule the canonical pages stay silent on the collision and the defect is filed as [Coordinate colliding onboarding prompts](../product-opportunities/coordinate-colliding-onboarding-prompts.md). **Page changes:** [Token panel](../../governance/token-panel.md) now states the separate Lock to Vote plugin's lock-on-connect trigger beside its adjacent nudges — deliberate connection, positive plugin-token balance, nothing locked with the body's lock manager, never leading into delegation — and the returning-holder consequence of the deliberate-versus-silent connection rule. [Choose a voting-power mechanism for token governance](../../guides/choose-token-voting-power-mechanism.md) states the participation-denominator difference — Token Voting weighs a proposal against the supply recorded with its own settings at creation, Lock to Vote reads live total supply, so the same percentage is a fixed bar under one plugin and a moving target under the other — and returns to draft for that addition. [Gauge voting](../../governance/gauge-voting.md) gains lending-market gauge registration: its own Gauge Registrar contract and `GAUGE_REGISTRAR_ROLE`, register/unregister identified by qiToken, incentive type, and reward controller, execution reverting during an active vote window, on the partially-supported footing — and the pass corrected a live claim, the gauge admin permission string being `GAUGE_ADMIN`, not the `GAUGE_ADMIN_ROLE` the page asserted. [Capital Distributor](../../treasury/capital-distributor.md) gains the lock-compounding payout mode: chosen by the campaign at creation, surfaced only for accounts wired with a voting-escrow resolver, warning rather than hiding when no escrow address resolves, the claimant's payout-address step unchanged. A second candidate came out of the unlock verification: [Surface unlock eligibility before the attempt](../product-opportunities/surface-unlock-eligibility-upfront.md) — the app simulates the Lock to Vote unlock on load, discards the verdict, and shows a fixed after-the-fact message that can misattribute the cause. Scope held: no exit-queue math, curves, checkpointing, or capital-flow material pulled in. **Board delta:** task retired — document-token-holder-prompts-and-reward-routes; opportunities added — coordinate-colliding-onboarding-prompts and surface-unlock-eligibility-upfront; draft added — choose-token-voting-power-mechanism (re-drafted for the denominator addition, 73 → 74); no owner ask, page question, or knowledge gap added or resolved.

- **2026-08-05** — Closed out document-interrupted-and-resumed-flows at `app@122f1bd1`, run as four parallel writer passes (one per page; Sonnet for the dialog census, Opus for the rest) with the lead session as integrator, followed by one adversarial Opus review whose surviving findings — seven blockers, five should-fixes, four nits — were applied in a single fix pass. The audit's raw evidence files were already cleared, so every claim was re-derived from source, and the task's own framing lost four points to that re-derivation. The pass landed concurrently with the day's ontology reshape and was merged onto it; the one placement it changed is that the in-flight conflict rule, which the task aimed at the proposal page, now lives where the reshape moved creation: [proposal creation](../../governance/proposal-creation.md).

  **What the pages now state.** [Wizard](../../application/wizard.md): one exit guard at the shared wizard root, inherited by both container families — the full-screen flows *and* the dialog wizards, so the task's "all five wizards" undercounted its own finding — challenging the navigation close control and in-app links, browser Back, and reload or tab close once the form is dirty, through the browser's own prompts rather than the product's [dialog taxonomy](../design/dialog-taxonomy.md); it releases when the submission dialog reaches its leave-from state, and the direct-creation dialog alone re-arms on a failed send. Outside-click refusal on dialog wizards is attributed to the app: at gov-ui-kit 2.9.0, the version the app builds against, the library's default is to dismiss — inverting the audit note that had that suppression as the library's own default. [Transaction submission stepper](../../application/submitting-a-transaction.md): the base's first chain-mismatch statement — detection against the transaction's own chain, an `info`-severity disclosure deliberately outside the warning/critical vocabulary, resolution inside **Approve/sign** before anything reaches the wallet, and the stale-chain refusal being the app's rather than the wallet's — plus a resumption section: submissions remembered against the action's identity, resumption automatic rather than offered (the task's "offered for resumption" is refuted for this page; the only genuine offer is proposal creation's), only a broadcast send surviving a reload, four suppression mechanisms retiring stale attempts, a reload-resumed send able to watch but not re-send, and new-versus-resumed as first-class states whose stuck-transaction clock runs from the original broadcast. [Dialog taxonomy](../design/dialog-taxonomy.md): 28 of 84 dialogs across 19 registries declare a wallet requirement (recounted exactly, matching the audit), enforced by skip-render and close-on-genuine-disconnect with a reconnection grace; the focus trap is a library default the app switches off statically for exactly two dialogs, and only the connect-wallet dialog also tracks the connector's lifecycle, releasing its modal block while the connector's UI is open — the task's "two dialogs release in step with the connection lifecycle" conflated the static flag with the dynamic tracking. [Proposal creation](../../governance/proposal-creation.md): the in-flight conflict check at publish — scoped to the same account and process, keyed on a content identity that deliberately excludes schedule (a timing-only edit resumes the earlier attempt as it was), stopping a second publish at a warning whose forward action supersedes the pending creation and whose resume alternative is offered only while the dialog's inputs are still held in memory, degrading to a plain dismiss after a reload; the check is the wizard's own, single-tab, and not run by flows that create proposals under the hood.

  **Cross-page corrections made while integrating**, both pre-existing: the stepper's leave-early control is labeled **Continue in background** (two page mentions said "Proceed anyway"), and direct transaction creation does not run the four-phase stepper — its dialog is a two-step send with no resumption memory, now stated on [create transaction](../../treasury/create-transaction.md) and [full-screen wizard](../../application/wizard.md#full-screen-wizard), which both said it follows the shared stepper; it does share the stepper's chain-mismatch disclosure and pre-signing switch. A fifth full-screen wizard exists in source — capital flow's Create policy — and stays off the canonical pages per the publication boundary and [product scope exclusions](./product-scope-exclusions.md). One adversarial finding was declined: linking the wizard page to its opportunity entry would break the board's one-way discipline.

  **Recorded, not filed:** the day-long resumption TTL ends silently; the conflict warning's forward action clears every matching record while showing one, so a multi-conflict case collapses; under-the-hood proposal creations populate records the wizard's check can trip over without running the check themselves; the warning copy says "this proposal" where it is by construction an edited one; a stale code comment promises a post-reload transaction link that was never built; the support-chat context consumes pending-transaction state. **Board delta:** task retired — document-interrupted-and-resumed-flows (row removed, file deleted); owner ask added — rule on the wizard exit prompt's chrome and reach; opportunity added — [keep the wizard exit guard armed](../product-opportunities/keep-the-wizard-exit-guard-armed.md) (candidate, design: a nested dialog wizard disarms its parent's in-app-exit guard, a never-removed history sentinel, both guard halves untested); page questions added — [alert severity](../design/alert-severity.md) (whether the chain-mismatch `info` disclosure admits a third severity) and [transaction submission stepper](../../application/submitting-a-transaction.md) (whether the single-tab pending-transaction memory is intended, flagged by two independent readings); no gap opened, closed, or narrowed; drafts unchanged at 73, seven rewritten in place.

- **2026-08-05** — Split the single 73-page owner-review ask into two dependency-aware passes after a filtered graph analysis and three independent reviews of the cohort and workflow. The pre-audit cohort is nine **semantic anchors**: Value proposition and Platform design principles as the product-intent roots, plus Account, Plugin, Governance process, Body, Action, Proposal, and Action builder as the shared semantic hubs. The graph count excludes log, backlog, task, index, planning-overlay, duplicate-link, and upstream noise; 44 of the other 64 current drafts directly depend on at least one anchor. The owner reviews this fixed set only after the six source-mining tasks and repository-wide terminology changes settle, so the purpose/topology and section-fit audits can consume approved meaning rather than infer it. The later owner pass is deliberately query-driven: it reviews every page still carrying `status: draft`, including pages created, split, or substantively reopened by the audits. Both reviews remain board-owned phases rather than task entries because draft status is the source of truth. **Board delta:** owner ask split and staged — semantic-anchor review before the structural audits, remaining-draft review after them; task state changed — audit-page-purpose-and-repository-topology from `ready` to `blocked` on the source, terminology, and anchor-review gates; audit-section-fit-and-location refined to preserve approved semantics and re-draft substantively changed pages; no draft, page question, opportunity, or knowledge gap changed.

New [Product scope exclusions](./product-scope-exclusions.md) is the source of truth for implemented surfaces that are not live; it currently records automated capital flows/policies and the in-app support channel, with documentation disposition, revisit trigger, and an optional product-opportunity link. [WORKFLOW.md](../../WORKFLOW.md) now owns only how rows enter and leave that reference. The linked-account helper was removed from exclusions after source-history verification: `linkedAccountDisplayUtils` has had no production caller since its original SubDAO feature commit, while the live assets and transactions selector labels the primary and linked accounts through `useDaoFilterUrlParam`. - **2026-08-05** — Turned the repository-wide `DAO` terminology audit into review-dao-terminology-violations, a 39-item linked owner-review ledger, then corrected the task-state model after the owner identified that actionable owner work had been mislabeled as blocked. The audit task resolves the prior report’s ambiguous conclusion: the read-only protocol submodule has no identified violations because its uses name the OSx DAO contract or explicit token-governance cases, while the platform value proposition, terminology policy, account presentation, account-scoped product copy, navigation, and maintenance references do contain violations. Exact identifiers and historical/source captures remain outside the rewrite set. All task entries now carry `next_actor`; the DAO review is `status: ready` / `next_actor: owner`, with a visible owner action rather than a trigger. **Board delta:** task added — review-dao-terminology-violations (ready; next actor: owner); workflow and task metadata migrated; owner ask added — disposition all 39 ledger items; no task retired and no draft, page question, or knowledge gap changed.

- **2026-08-05** — Set the publication boundary from the product owner's ruling on the codebase audit's four built-but-unreachable findings. Canonical product documentation now covers a feature only after the owner confirms it is live; partially implemented, in-development, local/preview-only, and unused source surfaces stay out until launch, while “partially supported” remains a valid documented state for a live team-assisted capability. [WORKFLOW.md](../../WORKFLOW.md) carries the maintainer-only exclusions: automated capital flows/policies and the in-app support channel are not live, and the tested four-tier linked-account/plugin display-name helper has no production caller and therefore defines no current naming rule. Linked accounts themselves are fully live; the audit's local-only flag observation was not their availability boundary, so their present-tense pages remain correct. The generic [reach-out pattern](../../application/getting-help.md#when-the-app-needs-the-aragon-team) is unchanged and unrelated to the in-development support channel. The exclusion was applied to queued work by removing automated-capital-flow controls as a proposed [DAO slots](../design/dao-slots.md) example, narrowing the remaining capital-flow and support references to explicit scope boundaries, and removing both from canonical [Source repositories](./repositories.md) prose. **Board delta:** owner ask resolved — all four built-but-unreachable findings; tasks refined — document-recognition-and-handoff-limits and document-token-holder-prompts-and-reward-routes; no task, gap, opportunity, page question, or draft added or removed.

- **2026-08-05** — Reworked the access-control sequence from the product owner's section-by-section review. [Authorization and execution model](../../access-control/authorization-and-execution.md) now defines access control, authorization, execution, effective authority, and administration in dependency order; treats target-local and shared policy as policy locations; and explains authority composition from a target-level role bundle through an account's aggregate downstream authority to the process that directs it. [OSx authorization paths](../../access-control/osx-authorization-paths.md) now identifies each DAO as its organization's shared access-control authority and introduces guarded plugin checks before ROOT administration. [Scoped authority](../../access-control/scoped-authority.md) now defines the execution-boundary scope directly, connects that scope to one account and treasury, states the selector condition's argument and value limits, and gives the OSx–OpenZeppelin comparison one home. The comparison records that both OSx and `AccessManager` provide shared access control while packaging the organizational account, treasury, execution route, and governance components differently. Eleven independent drafts were reduced through adversarial minimality, sequencing, and technical-truth reviews; [Gradual permission handover](../../access-control/gradual-permission-handover.md) remained unchanged because its existing sequence already applies scoped authority over time. **Board delta:** no draft, task, opportunity, owner ask, or page-question change.

- **2026-08-05** — Folded the product owner's Aragon Profiles clarification into [Claiming an Aragon Name](../../application/aragon-names.md#claiming) and [Delegate profile record](../../governance/delegate-profile-record.md). The deployed Member Registry is audited and authorized to create subnames under `aragon.eth`; its eviction and upgrade administration sit behind an internal Aragon-controlled address, so the page states the revocability and upgrade boundary without turning the exact internal holder into a product concern. ENS reviewed the delegate-record key and document shape and endorsed the approach, but the reference does not present that feedback as a general ENS standard or a headline product claim. Both page questions and their promoted backlog row were removed. **Board delta:** owner ask resolved — Aragon Profiles external-status facts; page questions resolved — Member Registry deployment and authority, and ENS review of the delegate-record shape; no task, gap, opportunity, or draft added or removed.

- **2026-08-05** — Processed the product owner's review of the app interaction-principles inventory into five draft design patterns: [normalize input without changing its meaning](../design/input-normalization.md), [show validation when it can help](../design/validation-timing.md), [preserve task context across dialogs](../design/dialog-continuity.md), [make user-supplied link destinations inspectable](../design/inspectable-link-destinations.md), and [use primary actions sparingly](../design/primary-action-hierarchy.md). The first two were rebuilt from first principles: normalize only proven meaning-preserving representations, and show errors at the earliest useful repair point. The owner's primary-action rule now owns both action scarcity and result-specific labels, replacing the narrower label-only candidate. Code verification corrected three audit claims: member-profile links already show their resolved destinations; proposal descriptions and delegate statements do allow arbitrary selected text to carry a URL through `TextAreaRichText`; and all live `SafeDocumentParser` callers pass strings while gov-ui-kit v2.9.0 accepts and sanitizes strings, so rich-content sanitization is a satisfied security invariant rather than an interaction principle or demonstrated vulnerability. Normal lock/wrap-to-delegation onboarding is explicitly sequenced and reported tested; only the internal note's exit-queue simultaneity case remains unverified, so document-token-holder-prompts-and-reward-routes now requires a combined reproduction or test instead of asserting that one prompt is lost. The reviewed inbox capture was fully mined and retired, and the two principles-page questions were resolved by filing interaction rules in the design pattern library rather than the platform-principles collection. **Board delta:** drafts added — input-normalization, validation-timing, dialog-continuity, inspectable-link-destinations, and primary-action-hierarchy; owner ask resolved — interaction-principle candidates; page questions resolved — candidate disposition and collection placement; task refined — document-token-holder-prompts-and-reward-routes; no gap or opportunity added.

- **2026-08-05** — Folded the product owner's token-governance, availability, and permission-management review into [Choose a voting-power mechanism for token governance](../../guides/choose-token-voting-power-mechanism.md) and [Governance designer](../../governance/governance-designer.md). The guide now starts from the economic claim whose reuse must be prevented, separates checkpoint-based Token Voting from custody-based Lock to Vote, makes the wrapper's pre-snapshot requirement and post-snapshot exposure explicit, and adds DeFi-position and veLocker routes without treating the underlying asset as the voting unit. Lock to Vote is available to everyone. Granular permission management is fully self-serve in Governance Designer, although complicated configurations may still warrant contacting the Aragon team. The application does not offer the token-voting approval threshold and installs it as zero. The advanced-governance guide and multisig-gate pattern no longer imply that a development environment or production handoff is required. - **2026-08-05** — Reworked [OSx authorization mapping](../../access-control/osx-authorization-paths.md) against a product-owner review of the page, run as five parallel analysis passes (framing, upstream-redundancy disposition, the `DaoAuthorizable` section, the Safe passage, the two closing value claims) followed by one adversarial review that caught a self-contradiction the first rewrite introduced. **The overarching correction the owner asked to be extracted: the page was describing OSx, which protocol-doc already does, instead of applying the model to it — so every passage now has to make one identifiable point in the model's vocabulary with a stated relevance to a platform reader, rather than chain protocol facts by association.** The page is reframed as the [authorization and execution model](../../access-control/authorization-and-execution.md) with Aragon's contracts substituted into its roles, opening on a role table (governor, account, intermediate guarded target, action target, shared configuration) whose third column is what each substitution obliges an organization to configure. The duplicated ASCII flow diagram is gone; the model page owns it.

  **What the review's specific objections resolved to.** *Guarded functions* are now framed as any contract deferring to a DAO, not as plugin functions: a `DaoAuthorizable` contract necessarily carries a DAO reference because the DAO is the permission manager, the *where* is the guarded contract rather than the DAO, and the two consequences are that naming a DAO grants nothing and that one table governs many contracts' functions. The `PermissionManager`-inheritance contrast, the `hasPermission` code block, and the whole nested-execution paragraph are cut as upstream mechanics. The Safe passage was three unrelated claims filed together; its one live point survives where it belongs — execute on the DAO is a grant on an **address**, so a Safe or EOA reaches `DAO.execute` through the identical check ([create transaction](../../treasury/create-transaction.md) is the surface) — and the section it was squatting in is now about routes alone. The route bullets keep the caller explicit per route as [target](../../governance/target.md) promises, name `DelegateCall` as the route the designer gives every plugin body rather than as an exception, and record that no `DAO.execute` check stands in that trace. The stable-address claim leads with when and why it matters — leaving an admin bootstrap, handing an action scope to a new process, while external contracts and treasury positions already point at the account — and this page now owns that elaboration, with [account](../../accounts/account.md) reduced to a pointer. The signals section names the linked-account acknowledgement grants concretely and says plainly that nothing ever asks the DAO about them.

  **Board delta:** owner ask added — rule on whether the permissionless-`GlobalExecutor` integrator consequence belongs upstream in protocol-doc; [access-control index](../../access-control/index.md) reworded off the rejected "guarded plugin functions" framing; the page remains `status: draft`; no task, gap, or page question added or removed.
